[TOOL_RESULT]
1	# RFC 0001: Workspace tool-requests file convention
2	
3	| Field | Value |
4	|-------|--------|
5	| **RFC** | 0001 |
6	| **Title** | Workspace tool-requests file convention |
7	| **Author(s)** | Jose |
8	| **Status** | Proposed |
9	| **Created** | 2026-02-28 |
10	| **Updated** | 2026-03-01 |
11	
12	---
13	
14	## Abstract
15	
16	This RFC specifies a **convention** by which any repository can declare **tool or feature requests** for consumption by aitools. Agents and humans interact with requests via GitHub (`gh` CLI or web). The convention is minimal, file-based, and assistant-friendly.
17	
18	---
19	
20	## Status of this memo
21	
22	This document is a **proposed** specification. Implementation in the aitools repository is optional and follows the project's normal decision process (e.g. maintainer approval, PR review).
23	
24	---
25	
26	## 1. Motivation
27	
28	- **Problem:** Repositories often need specific CLI tools (e.g. `typst`, `pandoc`) that are managed centrally by aitools. There is no standard way for an assistant or a human working in a repository to "request" a tool and for aitools to consume that request with proper context.
29	- **Goal:** Define a single, simple convention so that (1) repositories can maintain a list of requested tools in one place, (2) aitools can discover and process that list via GitHub, and (3) assistants (e.g. Cursor, Claude Code) can add requests in the repository and fulfill them in aitools with clear context.
30	
31	---
32	
33	## 2. Terminology
34	
35	- **Repository:** A GitHub repository that participates in this convention.
36	- **Tool-requests file:** A markdown file at a well-known path in the repository that conforms to this RFC.
37	- **Aitools:** The tooling and repository (`nobul-jose/aitools`) used to manage installed tools across machines. Consumes tool-requests files from other repositories.
38	- **RFC 2119:** The key words "MUST", "MUST NOT", "REQUIRED", "SHALL", "SHALL NOT", "SHOULD", "SHOULD NOT", "RECOMMENDED", "MAY", and "OPTIONAL" in this document are to be interpreted as in [RFC 2119](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2119).
39	
40	---
41	
42	## 3. Specification
43	
44	### 3.1 Repository side: tool-requests file
45	
46	3.1.1. A repository that wishes to participate in this convention MUST maintain at most one **tool-requests file** at the following path relative to the repository root:
47	
48	- `docs/aitools-requests.md`
49	
50	3.1.2. The file MUST be UTF-8 encoded markdown and MUST contain at least one **markdown table** with the following header row (column names are normative):
51	
52	```markdown
53	| Tool / Feature | Reason | Install / hint | Status |
54	|----------------|--------|----------------|--------|
55	```
56	
57	3.1.3. Each data row in that table represents one request:
58	
59	- **Tool / Feature:** Short name of the tool or feature (e.g. `typst`, `pandoc`).
60	- **Reason:** Brief explanation of why this repository needs the tool (e.g. "Use with Pandoc for PDF generation").

1	# RFC 0010: Python and SQLite Engineering
2	
3	**Status**: Final
4	**Date**: 2026-03-28
5	**Author**: Session Commander fbf7decb (with Commander Jose)
6	**Session**: fbf7decb-9284-466f-a6ec-50abcdca3d62
7	**Informed by**: harness-db.py (3009 lines), read-session-full.py (268), read-session.py (222), build-knowledge-db.py (1170), harness-db-schema.sql, setup-python.sh/.ps1, all hooks that detect Python, RFCs 0001-0009
8	**Relationship**: Engineering discipline for the harness's second language. Implements 0005-v2 (session intelligence data layer), feeds 0003 v2 (OL graph via knowledge_items), enables 0008 (verification via py_compile check).
9	
10	---
11	
12	## 1. Summary
13	
14	Python is the harness's second language after bash. It powers the entire data layer: session DBs, harness DB, KPI processing, event aggregation, knowledge DB, session transcript extraction, Datadog shipping, and the provenance system. Every Python artifact follows one rule: stdlib only, no external dependencies. sqlite3 is built into Python's stdlib. This is why SQLite won over every alternative.
15	
16	## 2. The stdlib-only principle
17	
18	No pip install. No venv. No requirements.txt. No external packages. Every Python script in the harness runs on a fresh machine with nothing but Python 3.8+ installed. This is a hard architectural constraint, not a preference.
19	
20	Why: hooks run in environments the harness doesn't control. A SessionEnd hook that fails because numpy isn't installed is worse than no hook. The harness must work on any machine where Python exists. The stdlib boundary is the portability guarantee.
21	
22	Exception: build-knowledge-db.py references sqlite-utils as optional enhancement. The core (FTS5 via stdlib sqlite3) works without it. sqlite-utils is evaluated but not required.
23	
24	Decision framework for stdlib exception: the tool must be (a) optional (core works without it), (b) evaluated via /tool-eval, (c) installed via uv tool install (user-level, no sudo), (d) documented as optional in the script header.
25	
26	## 3. The single CLI pattern
27	
28	harness-db.py is the single CLI for all DB operations. 3009 lines, 32 subcommands, growing. New functionality = new subcommand, not new file.
29	
30	Decision framework for when to add a subcommand vs a new script: if it reads/writes session DB or harness DB, it goes in harness-db.py. If it's a standalone tool with its own data (build-knowledge-db.py has its own knowledge.db), it's a separate script. If it's a session transcript tool (read-session*.py), it's separate because it operates on CC's data, not the harness's.
31	
32	Current scripts and boundaries:
33	
34	| Script | Purpose | Lines | Data boundary |
35	|--------|---------|-------|--------------|
36	| harness-db.py | ALL harness DB operations | 3009 | Session DB + harness DB |
37	| read-session-full.py | Full-fidelity session extraction | 268 | CC JSONL (read-only) |
38	| read-session.py | Text-only session extraction | 222 | CC JSONL (read-only) |
39	| build-knowledge-db.py | FTS5 knowledge index | 1170 | knowledge.db (own DB) |
40	
41	Splitting threshold: if harness-db.py exceeds ~5000 lines, consider splitting into harness-db-core.py and harness-db-provenance.py. But single-file has value: one import, one PATH entry, one version.
42	
43	## 4. SQLite patterns (canonical)
44	
45	Every DB connection:
46	```python
47	conn = sqlite3.connect(f"file:{path}?mode={mode}", uri=True, timeout=5.0)
48	conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
49	conn.execute("PRAGMA synchronous=NORMAL")
50	conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
51	conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON")
52	conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
53	```
54	
55	WAL for concurrent readers (MC reads while agent writes). NORMAL sync for performance. Busy timeout 5s for contention. Foreign keys enforced. Row factory for named access.
56	
57	URI mode `mode=ro` for read-only operations (export, status, list). Prevents accidental writes.
58	
59	CREATE IF NOT EXISTS throughout — safe to re-run. Schema applied via ensure_schema() which sets schema_version.
60	
61	CHECK constraints for all enum fields (status, category, severity, directive_type, priority, relationship, trust_level). Detection layer at the data level — invalid values rejected at INSERT.
62	
63	## 5. Schema management
64	
65	harness-db-schema.sql is CANONICAL (reference/, protected). harness-db.py contains the SAME schema as Python strings (SESSION_SCHEMA, HARNESS_SCHEMA). Both must stay in sync.
66	
67	Known weakness: two copies of the same schema. No automated sync check. Should be added to check-pre-commit (RFC 0008).
68	
69	Schema version in schema_version table. Forward-only migrations via ensure_schema(). No down-migration. If schema breaks: delete .db, re-init, import from JSON export.
70	
71	Decision framework for schema changes: add columns with defaults (non-breaking). Add tables (non-breaking). Modify CHECK constraints (breaking — needs migration script). Never remove columns.
72	
73	## 6. Python detection (cross-platform)
74	
75	```bash
76	PYTHON=""
77	if command -v python3 > /dev/null 2>&1; then
78	    PYTHON="python3"
79	elif command -v python > /dev/null 2>&1; then
80	    PYTHON="python"
81	fi
82	```
83	
84	macOS: python3. Windows: python (python3 not standard). Linux: python3.
85	
86	Hooks MUST handle Python absence gracefully (exit 0, silent skip). Scripts log error and exit 1.
87	
88	sqlite3 module verification before any DB operation: `"$PYTHON" -c "import sqlite3" 2>/dev/null`.
89	
90	## 7. The export pattern (Option B)
91	
92	Runtime (gitignored): .aitools/sessions/*.db, .aitools/harness.db.
93	Archive (git-tracked): .aitools/channel/running-estimate.json, .aitools/provenance-export.json.
94	
95	Bridge: harness-db.py export. Safety: refuses to overwrite larger file with empty data. Refuses to export when session has no meaningful content.
96	
97	Target (RFC 0005-v2): richer export with promoted knowledge_items, provenance_edges, KPI summaries. Import on SessionStart when harness DB missing.
98	
99	## 8. Error handling
100	
101	Hooks: ALWAYS exit 0 on Python/DB failure. stderr NOT suppressed for safety warnings. Pattern: `"$PYTHON" "$HELPER" <cmd> || true`.
102	
103	CLI: exit 1 on failure with descriptive stderr. Log to deploy.log via _log_detail().
104	
105	DB not found: hooks exit 0 (skip). CLI exit 1 with init instruction.
106	
107	## 9. Performance budgets
108	
109	Hot path (hooks): <50ms total. Python startup ~30-50ms. SQLite WAL read <5ms. Leaves ~10-15ms for logic. JSONL append (~0.1ms) preferred over Python subprocess for event emission.
110	
111	Cold path (SessionEnd): 2-10 seconds. Reads events.jsonl, computes metrics, writes harness DB, exports JSON, ships to Datadog.
112	
113	This hot/cold split replaced the failed synchronous telemetry (3 Stop hooks at ~200ms each with Perl regex).
114	
115	## 10. FTS5 and knowledge DB
116	
117	build-knowledge-db.py creates ~/.aitools/knowledge.db. FTS5 built into stdlib sqlite3. Tokenizer: porter unicode61.
118	
119	Architecture gap: knowledge.db and harness.db are separate. FTS5 search in one, provenance graph in other. No query federation. Target: merge or define spanning query layer.
120	
121	## 11. Version floor
122	
123	Python 3.8+ required. Features: f-strings (3.6+), walrus operator (3.8+), `from __future__ import annotations` (3.7+). No 3.9+ features.
124	
125	FTS5 in sqlite3 since Python 3.7.15+/3.8.12+. Verify at runtime via CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE attempt.
126	
127	## 12. Open questions
128	
129	1. harness-db.py splitting threshold (5000 lines?)
130	2. Schema sync automation (pre-commit check)
131	3. knowledge.db + harness.db federation
132	4. Python naming on Windows Git Bash (python vs python3)
133	5. Async layer for MC API queries
134	6. Test suite for harness-db.py (3009 lines untested)
135	
136	## 13. References
137	
138	Scripts: harness-db.py, read-session-full.py, read-session.py, build-knowledge-db.py, setup-python.sh/.ps1. Schema: harness-db-schema.sql. Rules: script-standards.md. Related RFCs: 0004-v2, 0005-v2, 0003 v2, 0007, 0008.
139

30	Pattern: JSON is fine for git-tracked archives (read once, export once). JSON is wrong for runtime intelligence written dozens of times per session.
31	
32	## 3. The SQLite Solution
33	
34	### Two-tier architecture
35	
36	**Session DB** (.aitools/sessions/&lt;prefix&gt;.db): per-session, WAL mode, one per CC session, written by that session's agents only. Created at SessionStart.
37	
38	**Harness DB** (.aitools/harness.db): cross-session, written at session boundaries only. Session index, KPI events, knowledge items (provenance), shipping state.
39	
40	### Why two tiers
41	
42	Session DBs eliminate write contention. Harness DB provides cross-session queries. The promotion pipeline (session -> harness) is the quality gate between fast loop and slow loop (RFC 0004-v2 section 11, safety mechanism #4).
43	
44	## 4. Intelligence Types
45

50	| Assumptions | observations (assumption) | `observation add --category assumption` | <1s |
51	| Observations | observations | `observation add "text"` | <1s |
52	| Incidents | deviations | `incident add "text"` | <1s |
53	| Messages | messages | `log --type sitrep` | <1s |
54	| Missions | missions | `mission start\|end` | <1s |
55	| Delegations | delegation_log | (hooks, RFC 0006) | 0 (automatic) |
56	| Commander directives | commander_directives | `directive add\|poll\|ack` | <1s |
57	| Commander feedback | commander_feedback | (via MC, RFC 0002 v2) | 0 (UI) |
58	| Events | events | (hooks, JSONL) | ~0.1ms (automatic) |
59	| Completed work | completed_work | (manual) | — |
60	| Requirements | hard_requirements | (manual) | — |
61	| Deviations | deviations | `incident add` | <1s |

1	# RFC 0006: Delegation Architecture

===== L2647 assistant 2026-11-16T00:10:03.412Z =====
[TOOL_USE Write]
{
 "file_path": "/Users/new-jose/repos/aitools/rfcs/RFC-0011-structured-reports.md",
 "content": "# RFC 0011: Structured running estimates, mission briefs and after action reviews\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|--------|\n| **RFC** | 0011 |\n| **Title** | Structured running estimates, mission briefs and after action reviews |\n| **Author(s)** | Session agent dbd05b0c, with the commander (Jose) |\n| **Status** | Proposed |\n| **Created** | 2026-11-16 |\n| **Updated** | 2026-11-16 |\n| **Issue** | [nobul-tech/aitools#180](https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/issues/180), body v5.2 |\n| **Session** | dbd05b0c-2365-4159-9976-bc64a2af874d |\n| **Plan** | `plans/structured-reports/implementation-plan.md` |\n| **Related** | RFC 0005-v2 (session intelligence), RFC 0006 (delegation), RFC 0010 (Python and SQLite engineering) |\n\n**Intent**: **Purpose**: Specify, as normative text, the three structured\ndocuments the harness uses to brief, report and track work: the mission\nbrief, the after action review (AAR) and the running estimate. Specify their\nshared schema, their files and names, the SQLite store every agent writes\nto, and the delegation duties that carry them. Settle the design points\nissue #180 left to this RFC. **Scope**: The schema (nodes, item types,\nfields), file locations and naming, the store and write rules, validation,\nlifecycle, cross-platform and security requirements, and the points settled\nhere. NOT the commander's decisions themselves, which this RFC cites and\ndoes not reopen (issue #180 section 1). NOT the work packages and batches\n(the plan). NOT the doctrine research (the issue comment \"Doctrine behind\nthe design\"). **Audience**: Whoever implements issue #180; every agent that\nwrites or reads a brief, an AAR or a running estimate; the commander,\nreviewing the points settled here.\n\nv1.0 (2026-11-16), session dbd05b0c.\n\n---\n\n## Abstract\n\nThis RFC specifies one schema for three documents:\n- the **mission brief**: what a delegate is to do, written by the delegating agent before launch;\n- the **after action review (AAR)**: what the delegate did against its brief, returned as its hand-back;\n- the **running estimate**: the session's living record, written by every agent in the session.\n\nIt specifies where each lives and how each is named. It specifies the SQLite session DB every agent writes to and how each kind of agent writes there, and the delegation duties that make delegates produce and read these documents. It turns the commander's decisions recorded in issue #180 (D874d-1 to D874d-53) into normative text, and settles the points that issue left to this RFC (section 4).\n\n---\n\n## Status of this memo\n\n**Proposed.** This RFC is normative for the implementation of issue #180.\n- **The commander's decisions** cited as `D874d-<n>` are fixed. This RFC does not reopen them. A point the implementer finds unclear goes to the commander as an RFI with a suggested answer.\n- **Points this RFC settles itself** are marked \"Settled by this RFC\" and listed in section 4. They are written as a version and reviewed after (D-DEL5).\n\n---\n\n## 1. Motivation\n\n- **No structured hand-back.** A delegate hands back a free-text list (`/delegate` \"Final response shape\" before v2.6). Nothing in it records what the delegate was told, what it did differently, or what it saw and left alone. Issue #177 (question 2) found that what a delegate learns after its merge has no home.\n- **No structured running estimate.**\n  - The estimate exists as an unmaintained stub, `.aitools/channel/running-estimate.json`, and a template that no code reads.\n  - The session DB (schema 4) holds intelligence items but no brief, AAR or estimate.\n  - RFC 0005-v2 section 4 lists both as gaps: briefings (\"Structured or freeform TBD\") and the running estimate (\"Aggregate from session DB\").\n- **No structure for the briefing file.** RFC 0006 section 4 (P1, briefing-first pattern) measured delegation quality rising from 2.0-3.4 to 4.0-4.8 once a shared briefing file was written, but never gave that file a structure.\n- **The order.** The commander's objective for issue #180: \"implement structured running estimates, briefings, aars\".\n\n---\n\n## 2. Terminology\n\n- **RFC 2119.** The key words \"MUST\", \"MUST NOT\", \"REQUIRED\", \"SHALL\", \"SHALL NOT\", \"SHOULD\", \"SHOULD NOT\", \"RECOMMENDED\", \"MAY\" and \"OPTIONAL\" are to be interpreted as in [RFC 2119](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2119).\n- **Governed terms** (`/glossary` skill): mission brief (synonyms: brief, delegate briefing), after action review (AAR), running estimate, deliverable, deviation, process deviation, observation, ambiguity, assumption, operational learning, proposal, blocker, session RFI, delegating agent, delegate, provenance. Their definitions are in the glossary; this RFC uses them in those meanings.\n- **Session agent:** the agent of the top-level Claude Code session.\n- **Delegate:** any agent launched by another agent, at any level.\n- **`<sid4>`:** the last four hex digits of the top-level session id (for example `874d`).\n- **Session id prefix:** the first 10 characters of the session id, hyphen included (for example `dbd05b0c-2`).\n- **`<mission name>`:** `<commanderid>-<n>-<slug>`, where `<commanderid>` is the delegating agent's own four hex digits (`/delegate` naming conventions).\n- **Session scratch directory:** `<repo>/.scratch/session-<session id>/` of the top-level session.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Specification\n\n### 3.1 One schema, three documents\n\n3.1.1. The mission brief, the AAR and the running estimate MUST share one schema. Every node and every item type is defined once, in this section, and MUST mean the same thing in every document that uses it.\n\n3.1.2. Each document uses the nodes marked below. A document MUST NOT contain a node it is not marked for.\n\n| Node | Mission brief | AAR | Running estimate |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `provenance` | yes | yes | yes |\n| `identity` | yes | | |\n| `intent` | yes | | yes (the session's) |\n| `objectives` | verbatim, with source | + status, how, refs, deliverables | the session's, + status |\n| `constraints` | yes | | |\n| `assumptions` | given | + end state | yes |\n| `decisions` | to honour | | yes |\n| `ambiguities` | already resolved, or flagged | found, how resolved | yes |\n| `deviations` | | yes | yes |\n| `observations` | | yes | yes |\n| `operational_learning` | carried forward | new | yes |\n| `proposals` | | yes | yes |\n| `rfis` | | yes | yes |\n| `missions` | | | derived (3.5.4) |\n| `assessment` | | | yes |\n| `rules`, `skills`, `access`, `name_prefix`, `duties`, `return` | yes | | |\n\n3.1.3. No document has a `facts` node (D874d-32), a `report_at_once_if` node (D874d-26), a `meta` node (D874d-19) or a `findings` node (D874d-22). A mission brief has no `conflict_priority` and no `permissions` node: they are standing duties in `/delegate` and the delegation rule (D-DEL13).\n\n3.1.4. `provenance` is every document's header (D874d-29). It records what the document is, who wrote it, when, and for which session and mission.\n\n3.1.5. Writers SHOULD emit the nodes in the order of sections 3.3.2, 3.4.2 and 3.5.2. Readers MUST NOT depend on key order. *Settled by this RFC (S-16).*\n\n### 3.2 Items\n\n3.2.1. Every entry of a list node is an item, and every item MUST carry these common fields:\n\n```\nid             item id (3.2.4)\ntype           objective | deviation | observation | ambiguity | assumption | decision |\n               operational_learning | proposal | rfi\ntext           the statement\nagent_id       who wrote the item (D874d-25): the session id for the session agent,\n               the subagent's agentId for a delegate, \"commander\", or a hook name\nagent_type     session | delegate | commander | hook\nmission        the delegate's mission name; empty for the other agent types\nsource.kind    commander | agent | telemetry | artifact | web\nsource.location  path:line | commit SHA | URL | transcript path + line\nquote          the source text verbatim; REQUIRED when the source is the commander's words\ncreated_at     UTC, Z suffix\nseverity       critical | high | medium | low   (OPTIONAL; deviation and observation only)\n```\n\nWho *wrote* an item is `agent_id`. Who *said* it is `source.kind` with `quote`.\n\n3.2.2. The type-specific fields are:\n\n```\nobjective  OBJ-n\n  text               verbatim from the delegation prompt (D-DEL8)\n  source             who gave it, and where (REQUIRED)\n  in an AAR or a running estimate, also:\n  status             accomplished | partly | not accomplished          (D874d-23)\n  how                a brief summary of how it was done, or why not\n  deviation_refs[]   DEV ids\n  observation_refs[] OBS ids\n  deliverables[]     what it produced: PR or issue URL, commit SHA, file path (D874d-34)\n\ndeviation  DEV-n                 acted on: done differently from what was given (D874d-22)\n  from.kind          brief | order | plan | rule | spec | standard     (3.2.3)\n  from.ref           where: OBJ-2, a brief constraint, plans/x.md step 3, a rule file and section\n  from.text          what it said, verbatim\n  text               what was done instead\n  cause              initiative | blocker | error                      (3.2.3)\n  why                the reason\n  effect             on which objectives, and how\n  objective_refs[], observation_refs[]\n\nobservation  OBS-n               seen, not acted on (D874d-20)\n  text               what was seen, without interpretation\n  evidence           quoted text, file:line, command output\n  incident           true when it is reported as an INCIDENT: marker\n\nambiguity  AMB-n                 (D874d-14, D874d-17)\n  text               the passage, verbatim\n  source             where: a brief section, an order, a rule\n  readings[]         the meanings it could have\n  resolution         the reading acted on\n  resolved_by        reason | read | test | delegate | rfi | assumption   (+ ref)\n  evidence\n  status             resolved | open                 (an open one is also an rfi)\n\nassumption  ASM-n                (D874d-15)\n  text\n  status             unverified | verified | falsified\n  confidence         low | moderate | high\n  evidence           what verified or falsified it\n  impact_if_false\n  ref                what settled it: a verified assumption points to a fact ('validated'),\n                     a falsified one to the contradicting observation ('invalidated');\n                     the glossary's assumption lifecycle, kept by D874d-43\n\ndecision  D<sid4>-<n>, or its record id\n  text, decided_by, date, quote,\n  status             decided | implemented | verified | deferred | reversed\n\noperational_learning  OL-n       (its own node, D874d-29)\n  text               what worked, what failed, what to do differently\n  deviation_refs[], observation_refs[]\n\nproposal  PRO-n                  (barrier analysis: D874d-1; no owner, no timeline: D874d-24)\n  what, why          other sources it rests on are cited in `why`\n  barrier_analysis   would it have prevented the deviation or the issue?\n  affected[]         files, rules, decisions it would change\n  deviation_refs[], observation_refs[]\n  disposition        proposed | accepted | rejected | deferred      (the commander's)\n\nrfi  R<sid4>-<n>                 (D874d-41)\n  title, question (with its context), suggestion, answer (verbatim, once answered),\n  status             open | answered | withdrawn\n```\n\n3.2.3. `deviation.from.kind` and `deviation.cause` take these values (D874d-29):\n\n| `from.kind` | What the agent deviated from |\n|---|---|\n| `brief` | The mission brief: an objective, the scope or an exclusion, a constraint |\n| `order` | An order given during the mission, outside the brief: the commander's prompt, or a message from the delegating agent |\n| `plan` | The steps or the sequence of a plan the work follows; a deviation of this kind is a process deviation (D874d-21) |\n| `rule` | A rule, a standing order, a CLAUDE.md file, or a skill's mandated process |\n| `spec` | A specification the work product must meet: a reference document, a schema, an interface contract, a test expectation |\n| `standard` | An outside authority: official tool documentation, a language or style standard, doctrine |\n\n| `cause` | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `initiative` | The agent chose to deviate, within the commander's intent (disciplined initiative) |\n| `blocker` | The given course could not be followed. A blocker is reported at once (D-DEL9); a denied write is one |\n| `error` | Done differently by mistake, and found later |\n\n3.2.4. Item ids are `<TYPE>-<n>` (`OBJ`, `DEV`, `OBS`, `AMB`, `ASM`, `OL`, `PRO`). Session RFIs are `R<sid4>-<n>` and decisions `D<sid4>-<n>` (D874d-41).\n- Once schema 5 ships, `harness-db.py` MUST assign item ids on insert, so parallel writers never collide.\n- Until then, the writer of a brief or AAR numbers its items from 1 within that document. *Settled by this RFC (S-7).*\n\n3.2.5. An item's status is never changed in place. A change appends a new version of the item (`version` + 1) and keeps the old one, so the record is never lost (\"provenance is eternal\", the commander, 2026-03-30). *Settled by this RFC (S-4).*\n\n### 3.3 Mission brief\n\n3.3.1. The delegating agent MUST write a mission brief for every delegate before it launches the delegate (D-DEL10). The brief MUST be written to `<session scratch dir>/<mission name>-brief.json` (D874d-36, D874d-42).\n\n3.3.2. The brief MUST be one JSON object with these top-level keys: `provenance`, `identity`, `intent`, `objectives`, `constraints`, `assumptions`, `decisions`, `ambiguities`, `operational_learning`, `rules`, `skills`, `access`, `name_prefix`, `duties`, `return`.\n\n3.3.3. The nodes hold:\n\n```\nprovenance\n  document           \"mission-brief\"\n  schema_version     1\n  version            \"v1.0\", \"v1.1\" ... (D-DEL5)\n  mission            <mission name>, the delegate's name\n  session_id         the top-level session's id\n  session_db         the session DB's path; empty until schema 5 ships (3.6.11)\n  session_scratch    the top-level session's scratch directory (every brief lives there)\n  written_by         {agent_id, agent_type, mission (when a delegate delegates)}\n  chain_of_command[] the transcript path of every agent above the delegate\n  created_at\nidentity             name, level, delegated_by, parallel_missions[] {mission, scope}\nintent               purpose, scope, out_of_scope[], audience\nobjectives[]         objective items: text verbatim + source                 (D-DEL8)\nconstraints[]        what the delegate must not do or cannot use: text + source\nassumptions[]        assumption items: what the delegating agent assumes; the delegate\n                     verifies or falsifies each\ndecisions[]          decision items: the decisions the delegate must honour, quoted\nambiguities[]        ambiguity items: points already resolved for the delegate, or flagged\noperational_learning[]  operational learning items, carried forward, each with its source\nrules[]              every file to read in full, line by line, by absolute path: every\n                     CLAUDE.md file, every rule, and the mission's references (S-8)\nskills[]             {name, use, override}: /delegate first, then the task skills\naccess               read_only[], writable[], scratch\nname_prefix          \"<mission name>-\"                                       (D-DEL1)\nduties[]             the duties specific to this mission (for example shipping a code\n                     change, D-DEL4); standing duties are not restated (D-DEL13)\nreturn               the AAR as the hand-back message, JSON in one fenced block\n                     (D874d-47, D874d-50); a file only when requested (3.4.7)\n```\n\n3.3.4. Only structured content goes in the brief. Anything unstructured, such as facts or background, MUST go in the body of the delegation prompt, outside the brief (D874d-32, D-DEL12).\n\n3.3.5. The delegation prompt MUST:\n- give the brief's path with the instruction \"Before any other work, read your mission brief in full, line by line: `<path>`\" (D874d-27);\n- carry the mission objectives verbatim, each with its source (D-DEL8, D874d-13);\n- tell the delegate explicitly what it reads in full, line by line (the brief and every file in its `rules[]`), and what it uses only as reference material, searched and never loaded whole (the chain-of-command transcripts, large registries and reports) (D874d-27).\n\n3.3.6. Every delegate launched through a workflow (each `agent()` call) MUST get its own brief, written by the delegating agent before the workflow runs, and its prompt MUST give the brief's path. *Settled by this RFC (S-3).*\n\n3.3.7. A blocker the delegating agent already knows at launch MUST be written as a constraint or in the prompt body, and the objective it affects MUST say so. The brief has no list of conditions to report at once: reporting a blocker at once is a standing duty (D-DEL9, D874d-26).\n\n3.3.8. A delegating agent that cannot write into the top-level session's scratch directory, because worktree isolation confines its writes (ASM-4), MUST write the brief into its own scratch directory, under the same name. The prompt then gives that path. *Settled by this RFC (S-9).*\n\n### 3.4 After action review (AAR)\n\n3.4.1. Every delegate MUST end its mission with its AAR as its hand-back message (D874d-47). The AAR MUST be JSON in the AAR schema, in one fenced block (D874d-50).\n\n3.4.2. The AAR MUST be one JSON object with these top-level keys: `provenance`, `objectives`, `ambiguities`, `assumptions`, `deviations`, `observations`, `operational_learning`, `proposals`, `rfis`. The first three answer \"what was supposed to happen\" (TC 7-0.1, 2025, 3-2 and 3-7).\n\n3.4.3. `provenance` holds: `document` (\"aar\"), `schema_version`, `version`, `mission`, `agent_id` (the delegate's own agentId, 3.4.9), `session_id`, `brief` (the brief's path), `created_at`.\n\n3.4.4. `objectives` MUST restate every objective of the brief, verbatim (D874d-19), each with its status, accomplished, partly or not accomplished (D874d-23).\n- Each objective gives `how`, cites deviations or observations, or both (D874d-23).\n- An objective that is not accomplished MUST cite the deviation that explains it.\n- `deliverables` lists what the delegate produced for it (D874d-34).\n\n3.4.5. The free-text lines of the old hand-back fold into these nodes (D874d-29, D-DEL11):\n- a `WRITE_BLOCKED` denial becomes a deviation with cause `blocker` and `from.kind` `rule`, reported at once (D-DEL9);\n- a `CONFLICT:` line becomes an ambiguity whose readings are the conflicting instructions, resolved by the D-DEL6 tier that won;\n- an `INCIDENT:` marker becomes an observation with `incident: true`;\n- PR URLs and files become an objective's `deliverables`.\n\n3.4.6. When a blocker stops an objective, the AAR records it in four places:\n- the objective's status is `partly` or `not accomplished`;\n- a deviation with cause `blocker` explains it;\n- an assumption is marked falsified, if a wrong assumption caused it;\n- a proposal or an RFI, if only the commander can clear it.\n\nThe blocker was also reported at once, with a recommended course of action (D-DEL9).\n\n3.4.7. An AAR file is written only when one is requested. It then MUST be the same JSON, at `<session scratch dir>/<mission name>-aar.json` (D874d-47).\n\n3.4.8. Once schema 5 ships:\n- every item of the AAR MUST also be a row in the session DB, written by the delegate as it works;\n- the delegate SHOULD produce its hand-back from its rows (`harness-db.py aar`, 3.6.10);\n- a delegate that cannot write the session DB because worktree isolation confines its writes (ASM-3) hands back its AAR only, and the session agent then writes its items, with `agent_id` taken from the AAR's `provenance.agent_id`. *Settled by this RFC (S-9).*\n\n3.4.9. A delegate MUST find its own agentId as `/delegate` \"Claude Code specifics\" documents: from a `TaskStop` error, or from its parent session's transcript (D874d-33).\n- This holds for the Claude Code harness. Other harnesses (Claude Code web, Cursor CLI, Cursor cloud agents) MUST be examined before they are relied on.\n- A delegate that cannot find its id writes its rows with an empty `agent_id` and its `mission`. The session agent fills the id from the AAR's `provenance.agent_id` on hand-back (ASM-1).\n\n### 3.5 Running estimate\n\n3.5.1. There is one running estimate per session (D874d-8). It lives in the session DB (D874d-35) and is written directly by the session agent and every delegate. It is not shared across sessions (D874d-9).\n\n3.5.2. Its export MUST be one JSON object with these top-level keys: `provenance`, `intent`, `assessment`, `objectives`, `missions`, `assumptions`, `decisions`, `ambiguities`, `deviations`, `observations`, `operational_learning`, `proposals`, `rfis`.\n\n```\nprovenance           document \"running-estimate\", schema_version, version, session_id,\n                     session_db, maintainer (the session agent), created_at, updated_at\nintent               the commander's intent for the session; main_effort\nassessment           is the session proceeding according to the commander's intent?\n                     (FM 1-02.1 \"running estimate\"; FM 5-0 table C-1 item 6)\nobjectives[]         the session's objectives: the commander's orders verbatim, with the\n                     transcript line, + status, how, refs, deliverables\nmissions[]           derived on export (3.5.4)\nassumptions[], decisions[], ambiguities[], deviations[], observations[],\noperational_learning[], proposals[], rfis[]\n```\n\n3.5.3. The session agent MUST update `intent`, `assessment` and the session's `objectives` at each prompt turn (prompt-turn checklist item 12) and after each hand-back. *Settled by this RFC (S-2).*\n\n3.5.4. `missions[]` is derived on export and never written. It has one entry per `<mission name>-brief.json` in the session scratch directory, with the `agent_id` and the objectives' status found on that mission's rows in the session DB.\n\n3.5.5. At session end, the SessionEnd hook MUST:\n- export the estimate to `<repo>/.aitools/channel/estimates/<session id prefix>.json`, which stays untracked: `.aitools/channel/estimates/` is added to `.gitignore` (D874d-44, D874d-52);\n- copy it to `<dotprofile>/running-estimates/<project>/<YYYY-MM-DD>_<session id prefix>.json` (D874d-52, D874d-53);\n- commit and push the dotprofile's `main` (D874d-51).\n\nThe copy and the push MUST run detached, the way `session-archive.sh` hands the transcript archive to `ait-harvest.py archive`, because Claude Code gives SessionEnd hooks only a short window. The next session's catch-up (`session-catchup.sh`) MUST recover an export or a copy that an abrupt exit cut off.\n\n3.5.6. The SessionEnd hook MUST stop writing the single `.aitools/channel/running-estimate.json`. That file and `.aitools/templates/mission-running-estimate.json` are retired. *Settled by this RFC (S-13).*\n\n### 3.6 Store (session DB, schema 5)\n\n3.6.1. The session DB is `<repo>/.aitools/sessions/<session id prefix>.db` (D874d-44, D874d-48), one per top-level session. The session agent and all its delegates, at every level, write to this one file.\n\n3.6.2. At session start, `harness-db.py` MUST compare the full session id stored in an existing DB with the starting session's id. On a mismatch, two sessions share the 10-character prefix: it MUST log an error and refuse to write into the other session's DB (ASM-6). *Settled by this RFC (S-11).*\n\n3.6.3. All items go in one `items` table, whatever the type and whatever the agent; there is no table per agent (D874d-25, D874d-31):\n\n```\nitems   id, type, text, status, severity, fields, agent_id, agent_type, mission,\n        source_kind, source, quote, created_at, updated_at, version\nrefs    from_id, to_id, kind (objective | deviation | observation | deliverable |\n        validated | invalidated)\n```\n\nThere is no session-id column (D874d-31): the file is the session, and the export adds the id.\n\n3.6.4. The type-specific fields (3.2.2) MUST be stored in `fields` as JSON text, parsed and validated in Python. Queries MUST NOT depend on SQLite's JSON functions, which the Python 3.8 floor does not guarantee (RFC 0010 section 11). *Settled by this RFC (S-5).*\n\n3.6.5. Writers MUST pass the DB's path; there is no `--session` argument (D874d-31):\n\n| Writer | Passes to `harness-db.py` | `agent_id` | `agent_type` | `mission` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Session agent | `--db <path> --agent-id <its session id>` | its session id | `session` | |\n| Delegate, any level | `--db <path from its brief> --agent-id <its own agentId> --mission <its name>` | its agentId | `delegate` | its name |\n| Hook | `--db <path> --agent-id <hook name>` | the hook name | `hook` | |\n| The commander (command channel) | `--db <path>` | `commander` | `commander` | |\n\nThe session agent knows its session id and the DB's path from the SessionStart hook's output (\"Session started: …\", \"Session DB: …\"; ASM-5).\n\n3.6.6. Connections MUST use the RFC 0010 section 4 settings (WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `busy_timeout=5000`, foreign keys) and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for writes. When a write still fails with \"database is locked\" after the busy timeout, the CLI MUST retry up to three times with backoff, then exit 1 with the error (ASM-2). *Settled by this RFC (S-10).*\n\n3.6.7. The migration from schema 4 MUST be forward-only, inside `ensure_schema` (RFC 0010 section 5), and tested on copies of the live DBs, as PR #181 was. It maps:\n\n| v4 | v5 |\n|---|---|\n| `observations` rows | `items` rows |\n| `attributed_to` | `agent_id`; `agent_type` is `commander` when `attributed_to` is `commander`, else `session` |\n| type `finding` | `observation` (D874d-28) |\n| rows of the v4 `deviations` table | items of type `deviation`, `from.kind` `plan`, `text` from the description, `effect` from the impact |\n\nA view named `observations` over `items` MUST stay, for existing readers such as `intelligence-stop.py`. The existing `missions` and `delegation_log` tables stay unchanged (RFC 0010: never remove columns), but nothing in this design depends on them. *Settled by this RFC (S-12, S-17).*\n\n3.6.8. `scripts/harness-db.py` is the schema's source of truth, and `reference/harness-db-schema.sql` is generated from it (PR #181). This supersedes RFC 0010 section 5, which called the `.sql` file canonical.\n\n3.6.9. `--session` MUST be removed in the same change that switches the hooks (`harness-db-sessionstart.sh`, `harness-db-sessionend.sh`) to `--db`, so no hook calls a removed argument. *Settled by this RFC (S-15).*\n\n3.6.10. The CLI gains:\n- `item add | update | list`;\n- `aar --agent-id <id>`, which renders a delegate's rows as its AAR;\n- `export --format json`, which writes the per-session export (3.5.5);\n- `validate --kind brief | aar | estimate <file>` (3.7).\n\nThe existing verbs (`observation add`, `ol add`, `decision add`, `log`, `knowledge add`) MUST keep working and write `items` rows. *Settled by this RFC (S-12).*\n\n3.6.11. Until schema 5 ships, nothing is written to the DB for these documents. `provenance.session_db` is empty, and the AAR hand-back is the whole record (AMB-6).\n\n### 3.7 Schemas, templates and validation\n\n3.7.1. The schemas MUST be JSON Schema files, and every document MUST have a template:\n\n| Kind | Files |\n|---|---|\n| Schemas | `reference/schemas/mission-brief.schema.json`, `reference/schemas/aar.schema.json`, `reference/schemas/running-estimate.schema.json` |\n| Templates (empty instances) | `reference/templates/mission-brief.json`, `reference/templates/aar.json`, `reference/templates/running-estimate.json` |\n| Prose reference | `reference/structured-reports.md` |\n\n`deploy-harness` copies the git-tracked `reference/` tree to `~/.aitools/reference/` read-only, so a delegate in any repo can read the schemas. *Settled by this RFC (S-1).*\n\n3.7.2. Validation MUST use the Python standard library only (RFC 0010 section 2). `harness-db.py validate` implements the subset of JSON Schema the schema files use: `type`, `required`, `properties`, `items`, `enum`, `additionalProperties: false`. No third-party validator is added. *Settled by this RFC (S-6).*\n\n### 3.8 Names and ids\n\n| Thing | Convention | Example |\n|---|---|---|\n| Mission brief | `<session scratch dir>/<mission name>-brief.json` | `874d-2-structured-reports-brief.json` |\n| AAR file (on request only) | `<session scratch dir>/<mission name>-aar.json` | `874d-2-structured-reports-aar.json` |\n| Session DB | `<repo>/.aitools/sessions/<session id prefix>.db` | `dbd05b0c-2.db` |\n| Export (project repo, untracked) | `<repo>/.aitools/channel/estimates/<session id prefix>.json` | `dbd05b0c-2.json` |\n| Record (dotprofile, tracked) | `<dotprofile>/running-estimates/<project>/<YYYY-MM-DD>_<session id prefix>.json` | `running-estimates/aitools/2026-11-15_dbd05b0c-2.json` |\n| Session RFI | `R<sid4>-<n>` | R874d-28 |\n| Decision | `D<sid4>-<n>` | D874d-53 |\n\nPaths MUST NOT contain spaces (D874d-53).\n\n### 3.9 Delegation duties\n\n3.9.1. These delegation duties carry the documents. They are recorded as decisions in `shared/rules/delegation.md` and as practice in `/delegate` (D-DEL7):\n\n| Decision | Duty |\n|---|---|\n| D-DEL8 | Mission objectives verbatim in the delegation prompt, recursively through the chain of command |\n| D-DEL9 | A blocker is reported at once, with a recommended course of action; this is a standing duty |\n| D-DEL10 | A brief for every delegate; the prompt gives its path and says to read it in full |\n| D-DEL11 | The final report is the AAR |\n| D-DEL12 | Unstructured content goes in the prompt body, not the brief |\n| D-DEL13 | Every delegate loads `/delegate` at start |\n\n3.9.2. The working copy of those files still names the brief `<sid4>_<mission name>-brief.md` in Markdown, and does not yet say the hand-back is JSON. Plan WP-2 brings them in line with this RFC (D874d-49).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Settled by this RFC\n\nEach point below is decided here, not by the commander, and is reviewed after (D-DEL5).\n\n| ID | Point | Settled as | Why |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| S-1 | Where the schemas and templates live (AMB-2) | `reference/schemas/`, `reference/templates/`, `reference/structured-reports.md` (3.7.1) | `deploy-harness` already copies `reference/` to every machine, read-only |\n| S-2 | Who updates the running estimate, and when (AMB-3) | The session agent, at each prompt turn and after each hand-back; the SessionEnd hook exports (3.5.3, 3.5.5) | The prompt-turn checklist already runs every turn |\n| S-3 | Briefs for workflow delegates (AMB-5) | One brief per `agent()` call (3.3.6) | The delegation rule covers workflows; a workflow delegate cannot ask for what it was not given |\n| S-4 | Changing an item's status (AMB-7) | Append a new version; never change in place (3.2.5) | Provenance is never deleted |\n| S-5 | Type-specific fields (AMB-8) | One `fields` column of JSON text, parsed in Python (3.6.4) | Python 3.8 does not guarantee SQLite's JSON functions (RFC 0010 section 11) |\n| S-6 | Validating against JSON Schema (ASM-7) | A stdlib subset validator in `harness-db.py validate` (3.7.2) | RFC 0010 section 2: stdlib only |\n| S-7 | Item ids before schema 5 | The writer numbers its items from 1 within the document (3.2.4) | No DB assigns ids yet |\n| S-8 | Where a brief lists the mission's references | In `rules[]`, which lists every file to read in full (3.3.3) | The spec's reading instruction (\"the references it lists\") had no node to hold them |\n| S-9 | Delegates confined to their worktree (ASM-3, ASM-4) | The brief goes in the delegating agent's own scratch; the session agent records the delegate's items from its AAR (3.3.8, 3.4.8) | `/delegate`: worktree isolation \"confines writes to the worktree\" |\n| S-10 | Lock contention (ASM-2) | Three retries with backoff after the busy timeout, then exit 1 (3.6.6) | A locked DB fails loudly instead of losing a write |\n| S-11 | Prefix collision (ASM-6) | Checked at session start; never write another session's DB (3.6.2) | 36 bits is unique in practice, but a collision would mix two sessions |\n| S-12 | Existing tables and verbs | An `observations` view over `items`; the old verbs write `items`; `missions` and `delegation_log` stay unchanged (3.6.7, 3.6.10) | RFC 0010: never remove columns; existing readers keep working |\n| S-13 | The old single export | Retired, along with its template (3.5.6) | Replaced by the per-session export and the dotprofile record |\n| S-14 | Briefs and AAR files reaching a tracked path | The harvester treats `*-brief.json` and `*-aar.json` as ephemeral (section 7) | The harvester copies every non-ephemeral top-level scratch file into the tracked `harvesting/` |\n| S-15 | Removing `--session` | In the same change as the hooks' switch to `--db` (3.6.9) | No hook may call a removed argument |\n| S-16 | Key order | Writers emit the spec's order; readers do not depend on it (3.1.5) | JSON objects are unordered |\n| S-17 | The v4 to v5 mapping | The table in 3.6.7 | No v4 data is lost |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Alternatives considered\n\n| Alternative | Outcome |\n|---|---|\n| One JSON file per session as the store | Rejected: one JSON file cannot take parallel writers safely (R874d-1); SQLite is the store (D874d-35) |\n| A table per agent | Rejected: tables would multiply with every delegate and queries would need unions; one table with `agent_id` (D874d-25, D874d-31) |\n| A Markdown brief named `<sid4>_<mission name>-brief.md` | Replaced by `<mission name>-brief.json` (D874d-42) |\n| An AAR file by default | Rejected: the AAR is the hand-back; a file only on request (D874d-47) |\n| Mission commands that keep a `missions` table | Withdrawn: each delegate finds its own agentId (D874d-33); the brief file and the delegate's rows carry the rest |\n| A `finding` type | Retired: what was acted on is a deviation, what was seen is an observation (D874d-22, D874d-28) |\n| A list of conditions to report at once in each brief | Dropped: reporting a blocker at once is a standing duty (D874d-26) |\n| A third-party JSON Schema validator | Rejected: stdlib only (RFC 0010 section 2) |\n| Committing the export in the project repo | Rejected: the export stays untracked and the record goes to the dotprofile (D874d-52) |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Cross-platform\n\n- **`harness-db.py`:** Python 3.8 or later, standard library only. It is invoked as `python3` on macOS and Linux and as `python` on Windows (RFC 0010 section 6).\n- **Hooks:** bash on every platform (`.claude/rules/cross-platform.md`, \"Hook portability\"). A hook MUST exit 0 when Python or the DB is missing (RFC 0010 section 8).\n- **Paths:** no spaces (D874d-53), forward slashes.\n- **The dotprofile copy** goes through `ait-harvest.py` (Python), which already archives transcripts on macOS, Linux and Windows. Its tests run in all three CI jobs.\n- **Finding a delegate's agentId:** verified for Claude Code only (D874d-33). In Claude Code web, delegates cannot delegate further (nesting depth 1), but briefs and AARs still apply. Cursor harnesses are to be examined.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Security and privacy\n\n- **Not in a project repo.** Briefs, AARs and running estimates carry the commander's words verbatim and the session's content. They MUST NOT be committed to a project repo, and the aitools repo is open-source. The project-repo export stays untracked; the record goes to the commander's dotprofile (D874d-52). The dotprofile already receives the full session transcripts (`ait-harvest.py archive`), so the record adds no new kind of exposure.\n- **Not harvested (S-14).** The SessionEnd and SessionStart harvester copies every non-ephemeral top-level file of a session's scratch directory into the tracked `harvesting/`; today it exempts only `package-lock*`, `node_modules*`, `tsconfig*` and `*.config.json` among `.json` files (`ait-harvest.py` `_is_ephemeral`). It MUST treat `*-brief.json` and `*-aar.json` as ephemeral. Until it does, a harvested brief MUST NOT be committed.\n- **No credentials.** Writers MUST NOT copy credentials or tokens into any item, quotes included.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Implementation\n\nThe plan, `plans/structured-reports/implementation-plan.md`, splits the work into batches of two or three files, each with its verification:\n\n| WP | What |\n|---|---|\n| WP-0 | This RFC and the plan (this change) |\n| WP-1 | Reference, schemas, templates, and the `validate` verb |\n| WP-2 | The harness text brought in line (D874d-49) |\n| WP-3 | Session DB schema 5, with the hooks' switch to `--db` |\n| WP-4 | Delegation-guard checks, the AAR check, the SessionEnd copy to the dotprofile, the harvester exclusion |\n| WP-5 | `/handoff`, `/mission-control` and `/aitool-continue`; the old export retired |\n| WP-6 | Release |\n\nThe implementing mission's brief is `874d-2-structured-reports-brief.json` in the session scratch directory of session dbd05b0c. The mission launches only on the commander's order (D874d-45).\n\n---\n\n## 9. Open questions\n\nNone for the commander at this time. The points of section 4 are open to the commander's review (D-DEL5). A point the implementer finds unclear goes to the commander as an RFI.\n\n---\n\n## 10. References\n\n- **Issue** nobul-tech/aitools#180, body v5.2: decisions D874d-1 to D874d-53 (section 1), naming (2), schema (3), store (4), assumptions and ambiguities (6), implementation (7). Comments: the commander's answers (2026-11-15), the March history, \"Doctrine behind the design\", \"Answers recorded\".\n- **RFCs:** 0005-v2 (session intelligence), 0006 (delegation, P1 briefing-first), 0010 (Python and SQLite engineering).\n- **Harness:** `shared/rules/delegation.md`, `shared/skills/delegate/SKILL.md`, `reference/framework-intelligence-types.md`, the glossary (`/glossary`), `reference/prompt-turn-checklist.md`.\n- **Code:** `scripts/harness-db.py`, `scripts/ait-harvest.py`, `shared/hooks/harness-db-sessionstart.sh`, `harness-db-sessionend.sh`, `session-archive.sh`, `session-catchup.sh`, `delegation-duty-guard.sh`, `workflow-delegation-guard.sh`.\n- **Doctrine:** TC 7-0.1 (2025), ADP 5-0 (2019), FM 5-0 (2024), ADP 6-0 (2026), FM 6-99 (2021), AR 11-33 (2022); report of mission 874d-1-aar-doctrine.\n"
}

 "content": "# Implementation plan: structured running estimates, mission briefs and AARs\n\n**Intent**: **Purpose**: Sequence the implementation of RFC 0011 into small\nbatches of two or three files, each with its verification, so the\nimplementing mission can ship it work package by work package and the\ncommander can see where it stands. **Scope**: The gates before the work\nstarts, the work packages WP-1 to WP-6 and their batches, how each batch is\nverified and shipped, risks and their fallbacks, and the status table. NOT\nthe specification (RFC 0011, normative). NOT the decisions (issue #180\nsection 1). NOT the implementing mission's brief\n(`874d-2-structured-reports-brief.json`). **Audience**: The implementing\nmission (874d-2-structured-reports) and its delegates; the commander,\ntracking progress.\n\nv1.0 (2026-11-16), session dbd05b0c. WP-0 (RFC 0011 and this plan) is done\nby this version.\n\n## Sources of truth\n\n| What | Where | Precedence |\n|---|---|---|\n| The commander's decisions | Issue nobul-tech/aitools#180, section 1 (`D874d-<n>`) | 1: fixed; not reopened |\n| The specification | `rfcs/RFC-0011-structured-reports.md` | 2: normative; its section 4 points are review-after |\n| This plan | `plans/structured-reports/implementation-plan.md` | 3: sequencing only |\n| The mission brief | `<session scratch dir of dbd05b0c>/874d-2-structured-reports-brief.json` | The implementing mission's objectives, constraints and reading list |\n\nWhen two disagree, the higher one wins (D-DEL6). A conflict found is\nreported as an ambiguity in the AAR, and a point that needs the commander\nbecomes an RFI with a suggested answer.\n\n## Gates\n\n| Gate | Condition | State |\n|---|---|---|\n| G0 | The commander orders the implementation launched (D874d-45: \"21) no\" for now) | closed |\n| G1 | The six working-copy files of session dbd05b0c are on `origin/main`: `shared/rules/delegation.md`, `shared/skills/delegate/SKILL.md`, `registries/glossary.json`, `.claude/rules/glossary.md`, `reference/framework-intelligence-types.md`, `ROADMAP.md`. The commander takes care of this (R874d-27). The same holds for this plan, RFC 0011 and the `rfcs/README.md` row | open |\n| G2 | RFC 0011 has an independent reviewer's verdict (issue #180, WP-0 \"Done when\") | open |\n\nWP-1 starts when G0, G1 and G2 are met.\n\n## How every batch is shipped (D-DEL4)\n\n- **Worktree and branch.** One worktree, `<aitools>/.scratch/worktrees/874d-2-structured-reports`, off `origin/main`. One branch and one PR per work package, each based on `main`: `feature/874d-2-wp1-schemas`, `-wp2-harness-text`, `-wp3-schema5`, `-wp4-hooks`, `-wp5-skills`, `-wp6-release`.  "content": "\"\"\"Structural self-check of RFC 0011 and its plan: S-n refs, clause refs, cited repo paths.\n\nUsage: check-rfc-plan.py <repo root> <rfc.md> <plan.md>\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nimport re\nimport sys\n\nroot, rfc_path, plan_path = sys.argv[1:4]\nrfc = open(rfc_path, encoding=\"utf-8\").read()\nplan = open(plan_path, encoding=\"utf-8\").read()\n\ndefined_s = set(re.findall(r\"^\\| (S-\\d+) \\|\", rfc, re.M))\nused_s = set(re.findall(r\"\\b(S-\\d+)\\b\", rfc + plan))\nprint(\"S-n used but not defined:\", sorted(used_s - defined_s, key=lambda s: int(s[2:])))\nprint(\"S-n defined but never cited outside the table:\",\n      sorted(s for s in defined_s if len(re.findall(rf\"\\b{s}\\b\", rfc + plan)) < 2))\n\nclauses = set(re.findall(r\"^(\\d+\\.\\d+\\.\\d+)\\. \", rfc, re.M)) | set(re.findall(r\"^### (\\d+\\.\\d+) \", rfc, re.M))\nused_clauses = set(re.findall(r\"(?<![\\w.])(3\\.\\d+(?:\\.\\d+)?)(?![\\w.]*\\d)\", rfc + plan))\nprint(\"clause refs not found:\", sorted(c for c in used_clauses if c not in clauses))\n\npaths = set(re.findall(r\"`((?:scripts|shared|reference|rfcs|plans|tests|registries|\\.claude|\\.github|\\.aitools)/[^`\\s<>*|]+)`\", rfc + plan))\nmissing = sorted(p for p in paths if not os.path.exists(os.path.join(root, p.rstrip(\"/\"))))\nprint(\"cited paths that do not exist (should all be new files the plan creates):\")\nfor p in missing:\n    print(\"  \", p)\n"
}

- `log`, `knowledge add`, `decision add`, `incident add` and `directive add` write `messages`, `knowledge_items`, `decisions`, `deviations` and `commander_directives`, not one shared table.

- The session-start hook calls `session start --id`, not `--session`.

So the RFC is wrong in three places: on how existing verbs map to `items`, on what retiring the old export affects, and on where `--session` actually appears.

**874d-6-standards-verify: 31 defects (4 high, 13 medium, 14 low).** The four high ones:
1. The RFC makes every list entry an item, but `rules[]` and `constraints[]` hold plain entries, so a validator would reject every brief.
2. The existing verbs write five different tables, so "they MUST write `items`" is wrong as stated.
3. Retiring `running-estimate.json` breaks the dashboards and two skills that no batch touches.
4. The always-loaded rule `aitools-workspace.md` still says the running estimate MUST be tracked in git, which contradicts D874d-52.

Notable medium ones:
- the plan's gate G1 claims your "dont worry about it" covered RFC 0011 and the plan, but it only covered the six files;
- the plan names a governed registry's JSON path, which the rules forbid in plans;
- the new Python test suite gets no CI step;
- the RFC's `--agent-type` flag and its writer table disagree.

RFC 3.2.2 even points a verified assumption's `ref` to "a fact", a type the enum cannot hold. Implemented literally, the schema-5 CHECK constraint would reject or drop the v4 `fact` rows.
OBS-4 medium | RFC S-17 says "No v4 data is lost", and 3.6.3 puts every item type, `decision` included, in `items`, and 3.6.10 makes `decision add` write `items` rows.
